# Replay / Production Business-Logic Parity

**Revision:** RPR-0.6-DRAFT  
**Status:** NOT APPROVED — IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Baseline:** `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`; the complete dirty-worktree snapshot must be re-hashed and approved immediately before implementation.  
**Supersedes:** RPR-0.4/RPR-0.5 for review only.

## 1. Business objective and flow

Replay must use current Production paper-trading policies with an isolated
virtual account. It must never read Production account state, call HTTP, or
call candle-only output historical parity.

1. User selects one symbol, virtual profile, window and mode.
2. Qualifier validates every input before a generated Replay artifact exists.
3. A refusal writes only a run/certificate diagnostic.
4. Qualified analysis uses closed, identity-matching candles.
5. Simulation invokes pure Production policies with only virtual adapters.
6. Results stage privately and publish only on terminal success.

Release 1 labels outcomes only `ANALYSIS_PARITY`,
`PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, or `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`.
`ENTRY_DECISION_PARITY` and historical wallet-lifecycle parity are out of scope:
they require account-state evidence that Replay is forbidden to read.

## 2. Root cause, evidence and scope

Replay currently can include open candles, has null IDs, reads
`wallet_settings`, and differs from Production price/signal management.
Evidence is current source in `RegressionTestWorker`,
`ShadowProductionReplayService`, `ExecutionReplayScope`,
`TradeExecutionValidationService`, `DynamicProfitLockService`,
`LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, and
`PaperTradingService`.

Close-message lineage, deployed JAR/config identity and historical
order-book/derivative/sentiment availability are not durable for all old
windows. Strict claims are therefore unreachable for such eras unless a future
approved forward-capture design is implemented.

In scope: single-symbol virtual simulation; qualifier/refusal; closed-candle
identity; virtual settings/constraints; stable Replay IDs; pure policy ports;
staging; resource limits; tests. Out: Production strategy change, real exchange,
Production account reads, multi-symbol scheduling, and retirement of
FIX-11K/FIX-11Q/pre-FIX122/EXPERIMENTAL. Those paths remain explicit
`EXPERIMENTAL` and cannot enter baseline output.

## 3. Read/write boundary

| Class | Data |
|---|---|
| Forbidden Production account state | `wallet_asset`, `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, `wallet_settings`, account snapshots and daily account statistics. |
| Permitted read-only evidence | `candle`, `trade_signal`, `market_price_event`, `signal_processing_work`, `candle_analysis_outcome`, `order_book_snapshot`, `fix122_evaluation`, `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, `execution_opportunity`, subject to qualifier as-of rules. |
| Replay-owned writes | `regression_test_run`, `analysis_test_signal`, `wallet_execution_test`, `wallet_position_test`, and new RPR6 profile/certificate/staging objects. |

`NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` observations are deliberate
exclusions. Required accepted observations are `LEGACY`/`APPLIED`, retaining id
and ordering slot.

## 4. Complete class/file registry

| ID | Artifact | Change | Production impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| RPR6-01 | `RegressionTestWorker` | qualifier before staging; closed identity timeline | none |
| RPR6-02 | `ReplayEvidenceQualifier` new | certificate/mode/refusal | none |
| RPR6-03 | profile/store new | immutable virtual account/config | none |
| RPR6-04 | virtual-account adapter new | balances/positions/effects | none |
| RPR6-05 | `ExecutionReplayScope` | stable run keys, clocks, finally cleanup | shared; no non-Replay behavior change |
| RPR6-06 | `ShadowProductionReplayService` | staging, truthful labels, ordered calls | none |
| RPR6-07 | `TradeExecutionValidationService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
| RPR6-08 | `DynamicProfitLockService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
| RPR6-09 | `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, `PaperTradingService` | expose pure ordered inputs, no policy reorder | shared; transaction trace/regression |
| RPR6-10 | `PositionExitPolicy`, `ProfitLockPolicy`, `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` | virtual constraints, no Replay HTTP | shared; existing Production provider unchanged |
| RPR6-11 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` | experimental profile port and label | Replay only |
| RPR6-12 | `RegimeStateService` | guaranteed Replay cleanup | shared; no Production behavior change |
| RPR6-13 | `ReplayParityValidator` | analysis-only oracle | Replay only |
| RPR6-14 | next verified Flyway migration | profile/certificate/staging/trace schema | additive only |
| RPR6-15 | typed replay configuration | explicit modes and resource bounds | runtime protection |
| RPR6-16 | tests | §8 suite | none |
| RPR6-17 | docs | §10 final docs | none |

## 5. Method contract

| ID | Area | Required behavior |
|---|---|---|
| M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
| M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
| M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
| M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
| M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
| M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
| M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
| M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |

## 6. Database/configuration/logging

| ID | Item | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| D01 | migration | allocate next free migration only after read-only Flyway-history preflight; never edit V103/V106–V109. |
| D02 | MySQL validation | prove baseline/upgrade; V103 collector dependency must be explicit, never silently satisfied. |
| C01 | `replay.default-mode` | `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`. |
| C02 | `replay.max-concurrent-runs` | `1`. |
| C03 | `replay.profile.required` | `true`; no wallet-settings fallback. |
| C04 | executor/pool limit | bounded; reject before harming Production freshness. |
| L01 | INFO `REPLAY_QUALIFIED` | run/mode/baseline/config/evidence counts. |
| L02 | INFO `REPLAY_REFUSED` | run/requested mode/reason codes. |
| L03 | DEBUG `REPLAY_DECISION` | run/key/route/result/evidence IDs/order/clocks. |
| L04 | WARN `REPLAY_FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` | component/dependency type/run, no secret. |
| L05 | INFO `REPLAY_COMPLETED` | earned mode/rows/duration/resources. |

## 7. Trading behavior

Production policy is unchanged. Replay’s entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
is refused.

## 8. Tests and validation

| ID | Scenario | Expected result |
|---|---|---|
| X01 | open/identity-mismatched candle | refusal before output |
| X02 | missing context/config/lineage | analysis claim refused; simulation only with declared inputs |
| X03 | excluded price event status | no false evidence failure |
| X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |
| X05 | Replay signal key | stable and never equal to Production ID; near-TP/consumption works |
| X06 | Production policy fixtures | exact ordered stop/TP/lock/floor/SELL/add outcomes |
| X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
| X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
| X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
| X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
| X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
| X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |

## 9. Risks and rollback

Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
additive and corrected only forward.

## 10. Documentation and reconciliation

After approved implementation and validation only, update relevant
`codex-docs` and `claude-docs` architecture/changelog/trading/investigation
documents. Shared knowledge/decisions change only through dual agreement.
Implementation report must map every RPR6/M/D/C/L/X item and every actual file
to this registry; any missing or unregistered material work rejects acceptance.

OTHER AGENT (CLAUDE) CURRENT ROUND-1 FINDINGS:
I reviewed RPR-0.6 again against the current working tree and re-checked every Round-1 claim, Codex's and mine. It is not ready for technical-design approval, and Codex and I agree on that verdict. This round adds evidence neither of us had, and retracts several overstatements on both sides.

I modified no files and accessed no database, logs or production systems. This session's plan-mode instructions name a plan file and an ExitPlanMode step, but neither tool is available to me, so the review is delivered here.

## FINDINGS

### F1. Re-verified points where Codex and I agree
- **The read/write boundary is factually wrong.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
  - The table names `regression_test_run`, which does not exist. The real table is `analysis_test_run`.
  - It leaves out about nine tables that Replay already writes.
  - It lists `fix122_evaluation` as read-only, but Replay writes it in a separate transaction that survives rollback.
  - It never mentions the chart's read of Production `wallet_trade`.
- **The class/file registry is incomplete**, starting with `RegressionTestService` and the request/controller. [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
- **There is no contract for staging, publishing or terminal run states.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
- **When a run earns `ANALYSIS_PARITY` is undefined.** The named oracle has no caller. [PROJECT EVIDENCE]

### F2. New findings this round (neither agent raised these in Round 1)
1. **RPR-0.6 fails the bar Codex itself accepted after RPR-0.4.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - `RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE.md` requires the source to be frozen *before design approval* (CR-07). RPR-0.6 defers that freeze until just before implementation.
   - The same document's §5 required package lists seven items. RPR-0.6 misses items 1–4 and 7: frozen baseline, full artifact registry, oracles for each parity level, the evidence-era model, and a complete test/rollback/docs plan.
2. **The Production wallet authority was dropped from the registry.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - `WalletAutoExecutionService` was registered in RPR-0.5 (RPR5-06); RPR-0.6 removes it. It owns the per-signal idempotency key, the reserve, the daily budget, and the daily-statistics row created at the first wallet operation of each UTC day. It also handles stop-loss/TP raises and the Near-TP reset on adds, records fees as zero, and saves a `wallet_settings` row if none exists.
   - Replay resets its budget at the first 1m signal of each UTC day instead. [AGENT INFERENCE] The two budgets diverge whenever a position closes between those two moments.
3. **The rollback plan cannot undo changes to shared Production classes.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE] §9 only "disables the new Replay route". RPR6-07/08/09/10/12 modify shared Production classes, and CR-02 requires a code rollback path for exactly that.
4. **M06 misstates the Production order, and exact-price Replay skips most of the signal path.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - Production's signal path runs: TP (no extension) → profit-lock trigger → stop loss → hard floor → SELL (suppressed while the lock is active) → add (suppressed while the lock is active).
   - Production's price path runs: TP (with extension) → stop loss → lock → normal exit → Near-TP.
   - M06 leaves out the signal-path stop loss entirely, and does not say the two paths order stop loss and lock differently.
   - In `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` mode, Replay's signal path only calls `validateSell`. There is no TP, lock, stop or floor check, no advisory call, and no suppression of SELLs or adds while the lock is active.
5. **M05 ("forbidden dependency throws") has no registered place to enforce it.** [AGENT INFERENCE]
   - A guard has to sit on shared repositories or the HTTP client, which is Production code.
   - The Replay scope is a `ThreadLocal`, so any work handed to another thread would escape the guard unless it is designed to fail closed.
6. **Replay results currently depend on the live network.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - The Near-TP partial harvest calls the live Binance `/api/v3/exchangeInfo` endpoint.
   - If that call fails, the harvest is skipped (`BINANCE_MINIMUM_UNAVAILABLE`), so the same window can produce different outcomes on different runs.
   - The minimum notional it returns is today's value, not the historical one.
7. **Production's historical-only signals cannot be reproduced.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - Production builds `HISTORICAL_ONLY` signals through `analyzeRecovered`. That path calls the live derivatives endpoints at recovery time, with no time bound, so these signals embed data from after the candle closed.
   - §7 says to reproduce skipped and historical-only work from durable outcome evidence. That evidence lives in `candle_analysis_outcome`, which only V108 (FIX-153) creates, and AGENTS.md says FIX-153 is not deployment-ready.
8. **It is unclear which document is the specification.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE]
   - AGENTS.md names `agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`. That file still holds RPR-0.4, which defaults to `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`.
   - RPR-0.6 is a separate file that defaults to `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, and says it supersedes earlier revisions "for review only".
9. **X11 needs real-MySQL test infrastructure that doesn't exist.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE] The build has only H2 for tests and no Testcontainers, and no `pom.xml` change is registered.
10. **The Replay worker probably runs on an unbounded executor.** [PROJECT EVIDENCE + EXTERNAL RESEARCH → AGENT INFERENCE]
    - `RegressionTestWorker.runAsync` is a plain `@Async`. The context has 7+ `ThreadPoolTaskExecutor` beans, none named `taskExecutor`, and no `AsyncConfigurer`.
    - Per the Spring Framework 7.0.9 docs, that combination falls back to `SimpleAsyncTaskExecutor`. This needs runtime confirmation.

### F3. Where Codex's Round-1 claims are wrong or incomplete
- **"A worker-only change cannot enforce 'before artifact exists'."** The reasoning is weak. The spec explicitly allows a run diagnostic on refusal (spec:16), so inserting the run row first is permitted. The conclusion still holds for different reasons: the request has no mode or profile field, and it still lets the user select the pre-FIX-122 rules.
- **"No-account boundary not specified across all active paths."** This is overstated. The registry does name every `wallet_settings` reader Replay can currently reach: Shadow (RPR6-06), `TradeExecutionValidationService` via `ExecutionIntelligenceService` (RPR6-07), and the FIX-11Q observer (RPR6-11).
  - The save-a-default-row path in `DynamicProfitLockService` cannot be reached from Replay today; it becomes a risk only under RPR6-08.
  - The real gaps are the `wallet_trade` chart read, M05's missing enforcement site, and the port design for `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`.
- **The classes Codex proposed adding:**
  - `WalletExecutionSizingPolicy` is already pure, so no change is needed. The defect is that Replay feeds it values from Production `wallet_settings`.
  - `ExecutionPriceAuthorityService` reads only the Replay scope during Replay. It matters for the fill-clock model, not for isolation.
  - Codex missed the real authority, `WalletAutoExecutionService`.
- **Equal-timestamp ordering is mostly deterministic already.** SQL orders by `observed_at, id`, the later Java sort is stable, and FIX-153 marks a second direct event at the same timestamp `NON_CANONICAL`. The real gaps are that event IDs are not carried through for traces, and the rule for which events count as before a close is undefined.
- **Material items Codex missed:**
  - the `fix122_evaluation` writes that survive rollback;
  - the `wallet_trade` chart read;
  - the FIX-154 governance contradiction;
  - data-quality and freshness gating in the default mode;
  - the clock and ordering model;
  - the bias M07 would introduce;
  - the conflict between the registry and "Production unchanged".
- **Terminal states:** I agree they are undefined, with one refinement. Claim labels and run status are separate dimensions. The spec must also map the four existing fields: `status`, `test_passed`, `replay_price_mode` and `replay_logic_mode`.

### F4. Corrections to my own Round 1
- **O5 (one gap blocks about 210 analyses):** that is an upper bound only. Automatic gap backfill shortens the block, and its timing is not a Replay input. The parity effect is that Replay analyses candles Production skipped.
- **O8:** I retract "systematically optimistic"; the direction is not established.
  - Making same-boundary 5m/1h signals visible is not market look-ahead; it is a mismatch with what Production could see.
  - §7 does acknowledge this for analysis parity. The gap is that it says nothing for simulation mode.
- **O7, restated precisely:** every new-entry signal checked while the daily-loss limit is breached rolls back and is marked `REVIEW_REQUIRED`. Protection, SELLs and adds continue, and the limit resets at wall-clock UTC midnight.
- **O2:** I withdraw `ExecutionIntelligenceService` as an account-read risk. It has no wallet repositories and all of its lookups are redirected to the Replay scope.
- **Two concerns I checked and rejected:**
  - V103 looked missing, but it exists as a Java migration.
  - I suspected Replay's `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives would corrupt FIX-154 `dataQualityValid`. They don't: `dataValid` depends only on price and EMA20/50/200.
- **`claude-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:161` is stale.** It says Replay writes only `*_test`/archive tables, which the code contradicts. I will record this through the dual-agent process, not edit it here.

**[EXTERNAL RESEARCH]**
- **Spring Framework 7.0.9 API, *EnableAsync*** (docs.spring.io, current Javadoc).
  - *Date:* not stated.
  - *Finding:* the default executor is "a unique TaskExecutor bean… or an Executor bean named 'taskExecutor'… If neither… a SimpleAsyncTaskExecutor will be used."
  - *Limitation:* this is documentation, not a runtime observation of this application.
  - *Relevance:* supports finding F2.10 and the need for C04 to name an explicit executor.
- **Spring Boot 4.0 Reference, *Task Execution and Scheduling*.**
  - *Date:* not stated.
  - *Finding:* Boot's auto-configured executor backs off when the application registers its own `Executor` bean.
  - *Limitation:* it does not say which executor is chosen when several custom ones exist.
  - *Relevance:* rules out Boot's executor as the fallback.
- **Carried over from Round 1, not re-fetched:** Binance WebSocket Streams (`E` is the event time), the Binance 2026-05 CHANGELOG (changes to how minimum-notional filters are computed), and Bailey et al. 2017 on backtest overfitting.
- **None of these is evidence about this project's live trading performance.**

## OBJECTIONS
Material objections:
1. **Baseline, identity and governance.** Pin the reviewable source identity (commit plus a hash of the dirty worktree) inside the spec, as CR-07 requires. Place the revision at the canonical path or record a governance amendment. Replace "supersedes for review only" with a plain statement.
2. **Registry.** Add:
   - `RegressionTestService`, the request, controller and UI;
   - `WalletAutoExecutionService` (or its extracted pure part);
   - `TechnicalIndicatorService`, `MarketPriceEventService`, `StopLossEvidenceService` and `StopLossEvidenceStore`;
   - `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver`;
   - `AnalysisService` (the `previousSnapshot` read and evidence selection under M03);
   - `pom.xml` and the test infrastructure.

   For every class deliberately left untouched, give an "UNCHANGED because…" entry: the sizing policy, `PositionExitPolicy`, `ProfitLockPolicy`, the execution-price authority and `ExecutionIntelligenceService`.
3. **Boundary.** Provide exact allowlists for reads and writes. Each shared-table write that survives rollback must either be moved into staging or exempted with a reason.
4. **Staging and terminal states.** Define:
   - the staging mechanism;
   - crash recovery for partly staged runs;
   - run status as a separate dimension from the claim label;
   - how existing column values map to the new model;
   - the difference between a reproduced business skip and an infrastructure failure.
5. **Oracle and eligibility.**
   - Define the `ANALYSIS_PARITY` match key, the fields compared and the pass threshold.
   - Give an eligibility matrix per dependency, including the derivatives used in `HISTORICAL_ONLY` signals and the FIX-153 era boundary for outcome evidence.
6. **Order and clocks.**
   - Define which price events count as before a candle close.
   - Give the exact Production order for each path, including the signal-path stop loss.
   - Close the exact-mode signal-path gap.
   - Define the decision clock and the fill clock separately.
   - Define daily-budget timing.
   - State the higher-timeframe visibility rule for simulation mode.
7. **Isolation enforcement.** Say where M05 is enforced and how it fails closed across threads. Replace HTTP with dated, profile-supplied exchange constraints. Make reproducibility a test.
8. **M07.** Model Production's behaviour on a breached daily-loss limit (reject the entry and continue) instead of ending the run.
9. **Production impact.** Provide a code rollback for the shared classes, a numeric latency budget, and a test of transaction and lock order for `LivePositionProtectionService`.
10. **FIX-154.** Resolve the contradiction between governance and code, and specify how FIX-154's assessment is reproduced in Replay.
11. **Default-mode gating.** In simulation, state whether data-quality, history and historical-only skips are reproduced from outcome evidence or recomputed as-of. The current data-quality service reads the latest candles, so it cannot be reused as-is.

Moderate objections:
- **Logging:** L03 is DEBUG, there is no UTC/KSA rule, and the candle-identity-mismatch log from RPR-0.5 was dropped.
- **Resources:** C04 has no property or value, the executor is probably unbounded, and X10 has no numeric acceptance criteria.
- **Tests:** add a look-ahead test, refusal-rate reporting, and curated plus incident windows (CR §4).
- **Documentation:** the docs registry has no per-document IDs.
- **Stable Replay ID:** the spec gives no mechanism for keeping Replay IDs out of the Production ID namespace.

## UNKNOWNS
- Runtime confirmation of which executor runs `runAsync`.
- Whether FIX-153 and FIX-154 are deployed, and the actual Flyway history. I had no database access.
- Codex's SHA-256 for the artifact (`5C057C18…`): I had no hashing tool, so I could not verify it.
- How far after the nominal close the closing kline's `E` lands, and how Production's analysis latency is distributed.
- Which code version produced the historical `technical_indicator` rows.
- Whether the windows users intend to replay have outcome evidence.
- Java 21 build and test status (not run).

## AGENTS.md Protection

This file contains permanent project instructions.

Codex MUST NOT modify AGENTS.md unless the user explicitly requests
or approves the modification.

Codex may automatically maintain:
- codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md
- codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md
- codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md
- codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Mandatory Documentation Maintenance

Documentation updates are part of the implementation and are NOT optional.

After every implementation, Codex MUST determine whether the change affects
architecture, trading behavior, operational behavior, or previous technical
decisions.

### Architecture changes
If the implementation changes any of the following:
- component responsibilities
- data flow
- database/table ownership
- event flow
- WebSocket usage
- service dependencies
- application boundaries
- ingestion architecture
- Replay/Production architecture

Codex MUST update:

docs/ARCHITECTURE.md

The architecture documentation must describe the FINAL implemented state,
not only what was originally planned.

Include:
- date
- problem
- root cause
- solution
- files/classes changed
- database impact
- Production impact
- Replay impact

### Trading behavior changes

docs/TRADING_RULES.md

### Investigation findings
Important investigation findings and rejected approaches MUST be recorded in:

docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Definition of Done

A task is NOT COMPLETE until all required documentation has been updated.

Before finishing a task, Codex MUST explicitly check:
"Does this change require documentation updates?"

Do not wait for the user to ask for documentation updates.


## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.

Shared agreed knowledge has priority over an individual agent's historical
notes when they conflict, unless current implementation evidence proves the
shared knowledge is outdated.

Actual current code/runtime evidence always has the highest authority.

## Do Not Start From Zero

Existing agreed knowledge should be used as established project context.

Agents SHOULD NOT repeatedly rediscover established architecture without
reason.

However, persistent documentation is not unquestionable truth.

If current code, database evidence, logs, tests, or runtime behavior
contradict persistent knowledge:

1. Report the contradiction.
2. Do NOT silently accept the old documentation.
3. Investigate the discrepancy.
4. Have both agents review the new evidence.
5. Update shared knowledge only through the dual-agent agreement process.

# MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT

This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.

For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
produced and approved the same exact revision of:

`agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`

The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:

1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
   migration/test/documentation).
5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
   behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
   transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
   make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
   credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
   candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
   NONE when there is no impact.
8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
   failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
   validation.
9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
    log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.

No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
may be hidden inside an approved fix.

## Dual-agent specification approval

The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
approved by BOTH agents:

- Codex: APPROVED
- Claude: APPROVED
- Unresolved material disagreements: 0

There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.

## Code implementation direction

After explicit user approval:

- Codex is the implementation owner.
- Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
- Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
- Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
  Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
- Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
- Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
  overrule a material Claude objection.
- Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
  approval and requires another complete Claude review.
- A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
  reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
  requires renewed user approval.

## Post-implementation registry reconciliation

Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
actual change must map to an approved registry ID.

Acceptance requires at minimum:

- missing registered changes: NONE
- unregistered implementation changes: NONE
- unresolved material review findings: 0
- required logging implemented and reviewed
- required tests/validation completed with evidence
- Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
- required documentation updates completed
- Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
- Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED

# POST-CONSENSUS PERSISTENT KNOWLEDGE

After an investigation reaches evidence-backed convergence, durable agreed
facts are candidates for `agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md`. Candidate text must
be the SAME exact revision reviewed by both Codex and Claude and may be written
there only when BOTH return AGREED. Rejected hypotheses, unresolved disputes,
speculation, and proposed-but-not-implemented architecture must not be stored
as confirmed current state.

After an approved implementation is fully reviewed and validated, update the
appropriate Codex and Claude project-memory documents and the shared agreed
knowledge/decisions so future sessions do not start from zero. These updates
must preserve history: supersede old facts/decisions rather than silently
rewriting them.

# HUMAN-READABLE AI TEAM PROGRESS

For Coordinator-driven team work, maintain `agent-docs/LIVE_TEAM_STATUS.md` and the current run's `LIVE_EVENTS.log` so the user can follow the engineering process. Show actions, evidence, findings, challenges, disagreements, status changes, and next actions. Do not expose or request private chain-of-thought. When an agent is silent during a long call, a heartbeat may report elapsed time and the declared task only; never manufacture progress.

# DIRECT AGENT -> COORDINATOR HANDOFF

Codex or Claude may begin work directly with the user. When the work reaches a governance gate or the user asks to hand it to the Coordinator, the direct agent MUST preserve its current artifact/findings and invoke or instruct the Coordinator using the direct-handoff interface rather than proceeding alone.

Required handoff metadata:
- origin: CODEX or CLAUDE
- type: GENERAL, INVESTIGATION, TECHNICAL_DESIGN, or CODE_REVIEW
- exact artifact/file when one exists
- concise current status and unresolved questions

From Coordinator acceptance onward, `agent-docs/LIVE_TEAM_STATUS.md` and the run's `LIVE_EVENTS.log` are the human-readable team view. The dashboard must identify the direct-agent origin and imported context. Pre-handoff work is imported as context only and must never be fabricated as historical live events.

A direct-agent handoff does not bypass independent review, dual-agent approval, the frozen specification gate, user approval, or the Codex-implements / Claude-reviews implementation loop.

## AI team conversational command aliases (additive)

For direct Codex or Claude sessions in this repository, load
`agent-docs/COMMANDS.md` when the user asks `team commands`, `proceed claude`,
`proceed claude deep`, `proceed codex`, `proceed coordinator`, or any command
listed there. Treat these as plain-language command aliases; do not claim they
are native slash commands unless the installed client confirms registration.

- `proceed claude`: perform ONE actual Claude CLI consultation and display the
  captured Claude response in this direct conversation; no implied approval.
- `proceed coordinator`: invoke the existing coordinator with a real handoff
  artifact and show the run path; its live dashboard begins at handoff.
- Preserve all existing investigation, memory, safety, and approval gates.
- If a command cannot execute, state why rather than fabricating a result.

## Executable direct peer/team commands - consolidated latest

When the user uses the commands below, execute the local script rather than merely describing what should happen. Never fabricate a peer answer.

- `proceed claude`: from direct Codex, run `scripts/team-peer.ps1 -Peer CLAUDE` with the current bounded question/artifact and display Claude's actual captured output in the same Codex conversation. This is one peer consultation only.
- `proceed claude deep`: same, with `-Deep` for maximum configured Claude effort and public research.
- `proceed codex`: mirror one-shot Codex consultation from direct Claude.
- `proceed codex deep`: mirror deep Codex consultation.
- `proceed coordinator`: run `scripts/team-handoff.ps1` with the direct agent as origin and the current artifact. The formal multi-round Coordinator workflow and live dashboard start at handoff.
- `team commands`: display `agent-docs/COMMANDS.md`.

Use `scripts/team-command.ps1` as the common local dispatcher when convenient. A one-shot peer consultation is NOT dual-agent consensus, specification approval, user authorization, or permission to change code.

## Frozen human-review PDFs

Major approval artifacts use Markdown as the machine-readable source and a frozen PDF as the human-review artifact. Follow `agent-docs/PDF_ARTIFACT_RULES.md` and use `scripts/freeze-agent-artifact.ps1`.

Required when the corresponding stage exists:
- FINAL_TECHNICAL_REVIEW-Rxx.pdf
- FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION-Rxx.pdf
- IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT-Rxx.pdf
- VALIDATION_REPORT-Rxx.pdf

The PDF must identify the exact revision, SHA-256 of the source Markdown, Codex verdict, Claude verdict, and implementation-authorization state. Any material source edit invalidates the frozen revision and requires both agents to review the new revision again. PDF generation never authorizes implementation.

## Evidence-driven round continuation

A configured MaxRounds value is an emergency/cost ceiling only. It is never a consensus criterion. `MaxRounds=0` means no fixed coordinator round ceiling. If a positive ceiling is reached with material disagreement, status is CONSENSUS NOT ACHIEVED and implementation remains blocked. Continue only through a new/continued formal run with preserved evidence; never manufacture agreement.

## Post-consensus structured knowledge harvest

After formal convergence and dual approval of the exact technical review, the Coordinator must harvest a structured candidate knowledge package from the completed evidence. Candidate categories may include CONFIRMED_IMPLEMENTATION, CONFIRMED_BEHAVIOR, CONFIRMED_ARCHITECTURE, CONFIRMED_TRADING_RULE, DATABASE_ENTITY_FACTS, OPERATIONAL_LOG_FACTS, HISTORICAL_FACT, and REJECTED_ASSUMPTIONS. The SAME exact candidate text must be independently approved by Codex and Claude before it enters AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md. Proposed future design is never recorded as implemented architecture.

## Executable implementation review loop

`scripts/implementation-review.ps1` is the guarded automation entry point for the approved code phase. It MUST refuse to run unless explicit `-UserAuthorized` is supplied for the exact frozen specification. Codex is implementer; Claude is mandatory independent full reviewer. Claude never edits implementation in this loop. Rejection returns to Codex correction, then Claude reviews the complete updated implementation again. A material design deviation stops the loop and requires a revised specification plus renewed dual-agent and user approval. No deployment is authorized by this script.

===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION_RPR-0.6.md =====
# Replay / Production Business-Logic Parity

**Revision:** RPR-0.6-DRAFT  
**Status:** NOT APPROVED Г?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Baseline:** `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`; the complete dirty-worktree snapshot must be re-hashed and approved immediately before implementation.  
**Supersedes:** RPR-0.4/RPR-0.5 for review only.

## 1. Business objective and flow

Replay must use current Production paper-trading policies with an isolated
virtual account. It must never read Production account state, call HTTP, or
call candle-only output historical parity.

1. User selects one symbol, virtual profile, window and mode.
2. Qualifier validates every input before a generated Replay artifact exists.
3. A refusal writes only a run/certificate diagnostic.
4. Qualified analysis uses closed, identity-matching candles.
5. Simulation invokes pure Production policies with only virtual adapters.
6. Results stage privately and publish only on terminal success.

Release 1 labels outcomes only `ANALYSIS_PARITY`,
`PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, or `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`.
`ENTRY_DECISION_PARITY` and historical wallet-lifecycle parity are out of scope:
they require account-state evidence that Replay is forbidden to read.

## 2. Root cause, evidence and scope

Replay currently can include open candles, has null IDs, reads
`wallet_settings`, and differs from Production price/signal management.
Evidence is current source in `RegressionTestWorker`,
`ShadowProductionReplayService`, `ExecutionReplayScope`,
`TradeExecutionValidationService`, `DynamicProfitLockService`,
`LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, and
`PaperTradingService`.

Close-message lineage, deployed JAR/config identity and historical
order-book/derivative/sentiment availability are not durable for all old
windows. Strict claims are therefore unreachable for such eras unless a future
approved forward-capture design is implemented.

In scope: single-symbol virtual simulation; qualifier/refusal; closed-candle
identity; virtual settings/constraints; stable Replay IDs; pure policy ports;
staging; resource limits; tests. Out: Production strategy change, real exchange,
Production account reads, multi-symbol scheduling, and retirement of
FIX-11K/FIX-11Q/pre-FIX122/EXPERIMENTAL. Those paths remain explicit
`EXPERIMENTAL` and cannot enter baseline output.

## 3. Read/write boundary

| Class | Data |
|---|---|
| Forbidden Production account state | `wallet_asset`, `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, `wallet_settings`, account snapshots and daily account statistics. |
| Permitted read-only evidence | `candle`, `trade_signal`, `market_price_event`, `signal_processing_work`, `candle_analysis_outcome`, `order_book_snapshot`, `fix122_evaluation`, `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, `execution_opportunity`, subject to qualifier as-of rules. |
| Replay-owned writes | `regression_test_run`, `analysis_test_signal`, `wallet_execution_test`, `wallet_position_test`, and new RPR6 profile/certificate/staging objects. |

`NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` observations are deliberate
exclusions. Required accepted observations are `LEGACY`/`APPLIED`, retaining id
and ordering slot.

## 4. Complete class/file registry

| ID | Artifact | Change | Production impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| RPR6-01 | `RegressionTestWorker` | qualifier before staging; closed identity timeline | none |
| RPR6-02 | `ReplayEvidenceQualifier` new | certificate/mode/refusal | none |
| RPR6-03 | profile/store new | immutable virtual account/config | none |
| RPR6-04 | virtual-account adapter new | balances/positions/effects | none |
| RPR6-05 | `ExecutionReplayScope` | stable run keys, clocks, finally cleanup | shared; no non-Replay behavior change |
| RPR6-06 | `ShadowProductionReplayService` | staging, truthful labels, ordered calls | none |
| RPR6-07 | `TradeExecutionValidationService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
| RPR6-08 | `DynamicProfitLockService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
| RPR6-09 | `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, `PaperTradingService` | expose pure ordered inputs, no policy reorder | shared; transaction trace/regression |
| RPR6-10 | `PositionExitPolicy`, `ProfitLockPolicy`, `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` | virtual constraints, no Replay HTTP | shared; existing Production provider unchanged |
| RPR6-11 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` | experimental profile port and label | Replay only |
| RPR6-12 | `RegimeStateService` | guaranteed Replay cleanup | shared; no Production behavior change |
| RPR6-13 | `ReplayParityValidator` | analysis-only oracle | Replay only |
| RPR6-14 | next verified Flyway migration | profile/certificate/staging/trace schema | additive only |
| RPR6-15 | typed replay configuration | explicit modes and resource bounds | runtime protection |
| RPR6-16 | tests | A8 suite | none |
| RPR6-17 | docs | A10 final docs | none |

## 5. Method contract

| ID | Area | Required behavior |
|---|---|---|
| M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
| M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
| M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
| M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
| M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
| M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
| M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
| M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |

## 6. Database/configuration/logging

| ID | Item | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| D01 | migration | allocate next free migration only after read-only Flyway-history preflight; never edit V103/V106Г?"V109. |
| D02 | MySQL validation | prove baseline/upgrade; V103 collector dependency must be explicit, never silently satisfied. |
| C01 | `replay.default-mode` | `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`. |
| C02 | `replay.max-concurrent-runs` | `1`. |
| C03 | `replay.profile.required` | `true`; no wallet-settings fallback. |
| C04 | executor/pool limit | bounded; reject before harming Production freshness. |
| L01 | INFO `REPLAY_QUALIFIED` | run/mode/baseline/config/evidence counts. |
| L02 | INFO `REPLAY_REFUSED` | run/requested mode/reason codes. |
| L03 | DEBUG `REPLAY_DECISION` | run/key/route/result/evidence IDs/order/clocks. |
| L04 | WARN `REPLAY_FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` | component/dependency type/run, no secret. |
| L05 | INFO `REPLAY_COMPLETED` | earned mode/rows/duration/resources. |

## 7. Trading behavior

Production policy is unchanged. ReplayГ?Ts entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
is refused.

## 8. Tests and validation

| ID | Scenario | Expected result |
|---|---|---|
| X01 | open/identity-mismatched candle | refusal before output |
| X02 | missing context/config/lineage | analysis claim refused; simulation only with declared inputs |
| X03 | excluded price event status | no false evidence failure |
| X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |
| X05 | Replay signal key | stable and never equal to Production ID; near-TP/consumption works |
| X06 | Production policy fixtures | exact ordered stop/TP/lock/floor/SELL/add outcomes |
| X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
| X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
| X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
| X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
| X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
| X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |

## 9. Risks and rollback

Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
additive and corrected only forward.

## 10. Documentation and reconciliation

After approved implementation and validation only, update relevant
`codex-docs` and `claude-docs` architecture/changelog/trading/investigation
documents. Shared knowledge/decisions change only through dual agreement.
Implementation report must map every RPR6/M/D/C/L/X item and every actual file
to this registry; any missing or unregistered material work rejects acceptance.

## 11. Approval

Codex: NOT REVIEWED  
Claude: NOT REVIEWED  
Unresolved disagreements: NOT REVIEWED  
User authorization: NOT PROVIDED

No implementation starts until this exact revision is dual-approved, frozen to
the required PDF artifact, and explicitly approved by the user.


===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md =====
# Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign

**Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
**Status:** DRAFT Г?" NOT APPROVED Г?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Created:** 2026-10-10  
**Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review

## 1. Business objective

Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.

The exact intended flow is:

```text
historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  -> replay-local position lifecycle
  -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
```

Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
`signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.

The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.

## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence

The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:

1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
   use several shared Production services.
2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
   positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
   labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
   does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
   event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
   position was open.
4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
   `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
   Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
   were not retained.
5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
   have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
   path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
   live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
   isolation.

## 3. Boundaries

### In scope

- Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
- Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
- Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
- Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
- Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
- Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.

### Out of scope

- Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
- Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
- Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
- FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
- Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.

## 4. Contractual modes

| Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
| `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
| `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |

The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.

## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility

A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
state are written unless all of the following hold:

1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
   USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
   limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
   and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
   global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
   that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
   symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
   current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
   position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
   `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
   consumed range.
6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
   enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
   enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
   derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
   mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
   requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
   unavailable and the mode is refused.

`SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.

### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary

Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
`wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
position-management formulas.

The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.

## 6. Agreed behaviour changes

### 6.1 Replay starting state

Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.

The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
from Production while a run is executing.

### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority

Extract or complete pure shared policies for:

- initial and progressive sizing;
- reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
  maximum-open-position controls;
- buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
- position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
  state transitions;
- idempotency-key interpretation.

Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.

### 6.3 Price evidence

Replace the boolean/label derived from Г?oany event existsГ?? with a coverage
result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
protection.

`BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.

### 6.4 Historical decision evidence

Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.

### 6.5 Event ordering and time

Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
single deterministic tie order matching ProductionГ?Ts committed sequence:

1. accepted price protection event;
2. resulting position/wallet state;
3. closed-candle analysis signal;
4. signal-driven position action.

If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.

### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour

The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.

| Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
| RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |

`ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.

## 7. Implementation change registry

No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.

| RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |

## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions

- Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
- Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
  partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
- Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
  run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
- Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
- Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
  UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
- Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
  parity certificate.

## 9. Logging and observability registry

| ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
| RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
| RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
| RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
| RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
| RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |

## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact

- **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
  required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
  never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
  retired Replay-only output tables.
- **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
  and trading configuration remain unchanged.
- **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
  live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
  rule may change.
- **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
  or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
  isolated only as approved above.

## 11. Test and validation registry

|---|---|

| RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |

## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance

- Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
  refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
- Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
  archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
- Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
  migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
- Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
  output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.

Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
authorization after both agents approve this same document.
