 157: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 158: 
 159: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 160: 
 161: ```text
 162: PENDING
 163:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 164:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 165:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 166: ```
 167: 
 168: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 169: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 170: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 171: 
 172: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 173: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 174: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 175: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 176: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 177: 
 178: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 179: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 180: 
 181: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 182: 
 183: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 184: 
 185: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 186: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 187: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 188: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 189: remains unchanged.
 190: 
 191: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 192: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 193: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 194: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 195: 
 196: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 197: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 198: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 199: 
 200: ## Deployment posture
 201: 
 202: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 203: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 204: 
 205: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 206: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 207: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 208: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 209: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 210: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 211: compile the Java 21 project.
 212: 
 213: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 214: without explicit user approval.
 215: 
 216: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 217: 
 218: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 219: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 220: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 221: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 222: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 223: 
 224: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 225: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 226: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 227: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 228: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 229: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 230: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 231: must stay `false`.
 232: 
 233: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
 234: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
 235: mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
 236: starting.
 237: 
 238: ### Shared Binance REST admission
 239: 
 240: `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
 241: immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
 242: response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
 243: the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
 244: calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
 245: of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
 246: 
 247: ### Outcome-schema startup invariant
 248: 
 249: The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
 250: terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
 251: though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
 252: runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
 253: table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
 254: when they attempt their first outcome write.
 255: # FIX-154 implementation update ƒ?" 2026-10-10
 256: 
 257: `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
 258: the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
 259: entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
 260: ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
 261: for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
 262: uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
 263: initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
 264: the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
 265: 
 266: `buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
 267: best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
 268: execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
 269: be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
 270: existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
 271: that guard has already rejected an add.
 272: 
 273: `GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
 274: blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
 275: 
 276: ## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation ƒ?" enabled 2026-10-10
 277: 
 278: In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
 279: five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
 280: missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
 281: the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
 282: An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
 283: plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
 284: dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
 285: ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
   1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10
   2: 
   3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
   4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
   5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
   6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
   7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
   8:   capped at 25%.
   9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
  10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  11:   never authorizes an add.
  12: 
  13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10
  14: 
  15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  16:   the data-quality gate.
  17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
  20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  21:   generation after a repaired quality window.

This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.

   9: 
  10: - System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
  11: - The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
  12: - Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
  13: - `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
  14: - 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).

  86: # SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
  87: 
  88: - Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  89:   This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
  90: - Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  91:   continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
  92: - Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  93:   evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  94:   to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
  95: - Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  96:   evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  97:   hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  98:   1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  99:   target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
 100:   drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
 101:   exit eligibility.
 102: - Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
 103:   or near-TP distance alone.
 104: - Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
 105:   with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
 106:   1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
 107:   protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
 108:   lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
 109: - Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
 110:   paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
 111:   comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
 112:   gross profit is a net gain.
 113: - Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
 114:   unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
 115:   near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
 116:   calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
 117:   pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
 118:   qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
 119:   a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
 120:   momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
 121:   and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
 122:   false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
 123:   reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
 124: - Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
 125:   entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
 126:   distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
 127:   evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
 128:   intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
 129:   floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
 130:   that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
 131:   improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
 132:   viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
 133: - Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
 134:   85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
 135:   quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
 136:   current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
 137:   unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
 138:   This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
 139:   Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
 140:   B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
 141:   must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
 142:   tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
 143:   use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
 144: - Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
 145:   from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
 146:   high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
 147:   `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
 148:   breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
 149:   fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
 150:   numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
 151:   length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
 152:   before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
 153:   out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
 154: - Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
 155:   before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
 156:   Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
 157:   order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
 158:   conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
 159:   with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
 160:   `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
 161:   breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
 162:   breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
 163:   avoided by A.

 165: # Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10
 166: 
 167: - Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
 168:   the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
 169:   database data, or Replay result was changed.
 170: - Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
 171:   `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
 172:   through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
 173:   `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
 174:   profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
 175:   classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
 176:   consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
 177:   visible, matching the documented live ordering.
 178: - Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
 179:   identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
 180:   manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
 181:   position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
 182:   reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
 183:   `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
 184:   real execution latency.
 185: - Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
 186:   persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
 187:   context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
 188:   stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
 189:   whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
 190:   are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
 191:   required historical evidence exists and is reported.
 192: - Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
 193:   `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
 194:   does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
 195:   protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
 196:   are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
 197:   should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
 198: - Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
 199:   parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
 200:   Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
 201:   Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
 202:   price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
 203: - Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
 204:   and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
 205:   evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
 206:   to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
 207:   scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
 208:   release-equivalence certificate.

  16: /**
  17:  * Thread-bound data/persistence override used only by Administration historical replay.
  18:  *
  19:  * The execution algorithms remain the production services. While this scope is active,
  20:  * their signal lookups are resolved exclusively from replay-generated signals as-of the
  21:  * current replay timestamp, and opportunity persistence is redirected to the supplied
  22:  * shadow sink. Production repositories are never read/written by the scoped execution path.
  23:  */
  24: @Component
  25: public class ExecutionReplayScope {
  26: 
  27:     /** FIX-109: Production parity is the default replay contract. Experimental mode is
  28:      * explicit so replay-only candidate rules can never contaminate a parity result. */
  29:     public enum ReplayLogicMode { PRODUCTION_PARITY, EXPERIMENTAL }
  30: 
  31:     private final ThreadLocal<State> state = new ThreadLocal<>();
  32: 
  33:     public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink) {
  34:         return open(runId, replaySignals, opportunitySink, ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY);
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink,
  38:                       ReplayLogicMode logicMode) {
  39:         if (state.get() != null) throw new IllegalStateException("Execution replay scope is already active on this thread");
  40:         List<TradeSignal> immutable = replaySignals == null ? List.of() : replaySignals.stream()
  41:                 .filter(s -> s != null && s.getGeneratedAt() != null)
  42:                 .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt))
  43:                 .toList();
  44:         state.set(new State(runId, immutable, opportunitySink, logicMode == null ? ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY : logicMode));
  45:         return new Scope();
  46:     }
  47: 
  48:     /** FIX-122: run-local executed boundaries, independent of Production ledger/history. */
  49:     public void recordStop(String symbol, long positionId, Instant at, String reason) {
  50:         if (!"STOP_LOSS".equals(reason)) return;
  51:         State s=required();
  52:         StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary previous=s.stops.get(symbol);
  53:         if (previous==null || !at.isBefore(previous.executedAt()))
  54:             s.stops.put(symbol,new StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary(positionId,at));
  55:     }
  56:     public StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary stopBoundary(String symbol, Instant asOf) {
  57:         StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary b=required().stops.get(symbol);
  58:         return b!=null && !b.executedAt().isAfter(asOf) ? b : null;
  59:     }
  60:     public Instant referenceInstant() { return required().reference; }

  65:     public long runId() { return required().runId; }

  67:     public boolean experimental() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.EXPERIMENTAL; }
  68:     public boolean productionParity() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY; }
  69:

  94:     /**
  95:      * Adds a freshly generated replay signal to the as-of context stream. Signal generation
  96:      * uses this incrementally so the production confluence/BTC services can resolve only
  97:      * context that had genuinely been generated by the current replay timestamp.
  98:      */
  99:     public void appendSignal(TradeSignal signal) {
 100:         if (signal == null || signal.getGeneratedAt() == null) return;
 101:         State s = required();
 102:         s.signals.add(signal);
 103:         s.signals.sort(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt));
 104:     }
 105: 
 106:     public List<TradeSignal> recent(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, int limit) {
 107:         State s = required();
 108:         Instant ref = reference != null ? reference : s.reference;
 109:         if (ref == null) return List.of();
 110:         return s.signals.stream()
 111:                 .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
 112:                 .filter(x -> !x.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(ref))
 113:                 .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt).reversed())
 114:                 .limit(limit)
 115:                 .toList();
 116:     }
 117: 
 118:     public Optional<TradeSignal> latestAtOrBefore(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference) {
 119:         return recent(symbol, interval, reference, 1).stream().findFirst();
 120:     }

 122:     /**
 123:      * FIX-141: Replay-side equivalent of
 124:      * TradeSignalRepository#findTopBySymbolAndIntervalAndCandleOpenTimeLessThanEqualAndGeneratedAtLessThanEqualOrderByCandleOpenTimeDesc.
 125:      * Selects by candle open time (close-time ordering), not generatedAt, so Replay cannot diverge
 126:      * from Production on which BTC snapshot it picks. candleOpenTimeBound is the caller's look-ahead
 127:      * bound (reference minus the interval duration); generatedAt must also be at or before reference.
 128:      */
 129:     public Optional<TradeSignal> latestClosedAtOrBefore(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, Instant candleOpenTimeBound) {
 130:         State s = required();
 131:         if (reference == null || candleOpenTimeBound == null) return Optional.empty();
 132:         return s.signals.stream()
 133:                 .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
 134:                 .filter(x -> x.getGeneratedAt() != null && !x.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(reference))
 135:                 .filter(x -> x.getCandleOpenTime() != null && !x.getCandleOpenTime().isAfter(candleOpenTimeBound))
 136:                 .max(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getCandleOpenTime));
 137:     }

 143:                 .filter(x -> x.getGeneratedAt().isBefore(reference))

 145:     }

 147:     public Optional<ExecutionOpportunity> currentOpportunity(String symbol, List<String> statuses) {
 148:         ExecutionOpportunity o = required().opportunity;
 149:         if (o == null || !symbol.equals(o.getSymbol())) return Optional.empty();
 150:         if (statuses != null && !statuses.isEmpty() && !statuses.contains(o.getStatus())) return Optional.empty();
 151:         return Optional.of(o);
 152:     }
 153: 
 154:     public ExecutionOpportunity saveOpportunity(ExecutionOpportunity opportunity) {
 155:         State s = required();
 156:         s.opportunity = opportunity;
 157:         if (s.opportunitySink != null) s.opportunitySink.accept(opportunity);
 158:         return opportunity;
 159:     }

 161:     public final class Scope implements AutoCloseable {
 162:         private boolean closed;
 163:         @Override public void close() {
 164:             if (!closed) {
 165:                 state.remove();
 166:                 closed = true;
 167:             }
 168:         }
 169:     }

 171:     private State required() {
 172:         State s = state.get();
 173:         if (s == null) throw new IllegalStateException("No Administration execution replay scope is active");
 174:         return s;
 175:     }

===== src/main/java/com/crypto/regression/service/ReplayParityValidator.java:1 =====
   1: package com.crypto.regression.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   4: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
   5: 
   6: import java.util.ArrayList;
   7: import java.util.List;
   8: import java.util.Objects;
   9: 
  10: /**
  11:  * FIX-091 / Fix 6: field-level pre-wallet parity comparator.
  12:  * It deliberately compares trading-intelligence state only; wallet execution is outside scope.
  13:  */
  14: @Service
  15: public class ReplayParityValidator {
  16: 
  17:     public Result compare(TradeSignal production, TradeSignal replay) {
  18:         List<String> mismatches = new ArrayList<>();
  19:         if (production == null || replay == null) {
  20:             mismatches.add("Production and Replay signals must both exist");
  21:             return new Result(false, mismatches);
  22:         }
  23: 
  24:         same(mismatches, "detectedRegime", production.getDetectedRegime(), replay.getDetectedRegime());
  25:         same(mismatches, "candidateRegime", production.getCandidateRegime(), replay.getCandidateRegime());
  26:         same(mismatches, "confirmedRegime", production.getConfirmedRegime(), replay.getConfirmedRegime());
  27:         same(mismatches, "selectedStrategy", production.getSelectedStrategy(), replay.getSelectedStrategy());
  28:         same(mismatches, "originalDecision", production.getOriginalDecision(), replay.getOriginalDecision());
  29:         same(mismatches, "finalDecision", production.getDecision(), replay.getDecision());
  30:         same(mismatches, "rawConfidence", production.getRawConfidenceScore(), replay.getRawConfidenceScore());
  31:         same(mismatches, "effectiveConfidence", production.getEffectiveConfidenceScore(), replay.getEffectiveConfidenceScore());
  32:         same(mismatches, "primaryBlockingStage", production.getPrimaryBlockingStage(), replay.getPrimaryBlockingStage());
  33:         same(mismatches, "btcContextStatus", production.getBtcContextStatus(), replay.getBtcContextStatus());
  34:         same(mismatches, "liquidityStatus", production.getLiquidityStatus(), replay.getLiquidityStatus());
  35:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthority", production.getEntryAuthority(), replay.getEntryAuthority());
  36:         same(mismatches, "entryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent",
  37:                 production.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent(), replay.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent());
  38:         return new Result(mismatches.isEmpty(), List.copyOf(mismatches));
  39:     }
  40: 
  41:     private void same(List<String> mismatches, String field, Object expected, Object actual) {
  42:         if (!Objects.equals(expected, actual)) {
  43:             mismatches.add(field + ": Production=" + expected + ", Replay=" + actual);
  44:         }
  45:     }
  46: 
  47:     public record Result(boolean matches, List<String> mismatches) {}
  48: }

  14: /** FIX-122: bounded, non-locking committed ledger reads; no managed-position row locks.
  15:  * STOP_LOSS is the terminal reason written by executeSignalLinkedExit. The distinct
  16:  * POSITION_STOP_LOSS and partial-harvest reasons are deliberately not included.
  17:  */
  18: @Service
  19: public class StopLossEvidenceStore {
  20:     private final JdbcTemplate jdbc;
  21:     private final TransactionTemplate read;
  22:     private final TransactionTemplate write;
  23: 
  24:     public StopLossEvidenceStore(DataSource dataSource, PlatformTransactionManager manager) {
  25:         jdbc = new JdbcTemplate(dataSource);
  26:         jdbc.setQueryTimeout(2);
  27:         read = new TransactionTemplate(manager);
  28:         read.setPropagationBehavior(TransactionDefinition.PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW);
  29:         read.setIsolationLevel(TransactionDefinition.ISOLATION_READ_COMMITTED);
  30:         read.setReadOnly(true);
  31:         read.setTimeout(2);
  32:         write = new TransactionTemplate(manager);
  33:         write.setPropagationBehavior(TransactionDefinition.PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW);
  34:         write.setTimeout(2);
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     public StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary latest(String symbol, Instant asOf) {
  38:         return read.execute(status -> jdbc.query("""
  39:                 SELECT id, executed_at FROM wallet_trade
  40:                 WHERE symbol=? AND status='EXECUTED' AND side='SELL'
  41:                   AND execution_reason='STOP_LOSS' AND executed_at<=?
  42:                 ORDER BY executed_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1
  43:                 """, (rs, row) -> new StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary(rs.getLong(1),
  44:                 rs.getTimestamp(2).toInstant()), symbol, Timestamp.from(asOf))
  45:                 .stream().findFirst().orElse(null));
  46:     }
  47: 
  48:     public void append(Long runId, String symbol, Long signalId, Instant at, String stage, String payload) {
  49:         // Separate audit transaction survives a caller rollback; it records an evaluation,
  50:         // never asserts that a BUY/SELL actually executed. No FK to wallet rows is acquired.
  51:         write.executeWithoutResult(status -> jdbc.update("""
  52:                 INSERT INTO fix122_evaluation(test_run_id,symbol,signal_id,evaluated_at,stage,payload)
  53:                 VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?)
  54:                 """, runId, symbol, signalId, Timestamp.from(at), stage, payload));
  55:     }
  56: 
  57:     public List<Map<String,Object>> replayDetails(long runId) {
  58:         return jdbc.queryForList("SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id=? ORDER BY id", runId);
  59:     }
  60: 
  61:     public List<Map<String,Object>> productionDetails(String symbol, Instant from, Instant to) {
  62:         return jdbc.queryForList("""
  63:                 SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=?
  64:                 AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=? ORDER BY id
  65:                 """, symbol, Timestamp.from(from), Timestamp.from(to));
  66:     }

  62:     public ValidationResult validateBuy(TradeSignal executionSignal, int entryQualityScore) {
  63:         ValidationResult structural = validateBaseSignal(executionSignal, true);
  64:         if (!structural.allowed()) return structural;
  65: 
  66:         WalletSettings settings = settings();
  67:         if (settings.isRequireNewBuyTransition()) {
  68:             TradeSignal previous = previousSignal(executionSignal.getSymbol(), EXECUTION_INTERVAL, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt()).orElse(null);
  69:             // FIX-112A: a bullish label alone is not a consumed entry. Only keep
  70:             // BUY_CONTINUATION protection when that exact previous signal has an
  71:             // executed BUY (Production) or executed shadow BUY (Replay).
  72:             if (isFresh(previous, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt(), ONE_MINUTE_TRANSITION_MAX_AGE)
  73:                     && isBullish(previous.getDecision())
  74:                     && entryConsumptionPolicy.resolve(previous.getId()) != EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED) {
  75:                 return ValidationResult.reject("BUY_CONTINUATION",
  76:                         "BUY was not executed because a recent bullish opportunity for this symbol was already consumed. "
  77:                                 + "A new WATCH/NEUTRAL/SELL -> BUY transition is required.");
  78:             }
  79:         }
  80: 
  81:         TradeSignal fiveMinute = latestAtOrBefore(executionSignal, CONFIRMATION_INTERVAL);
  82:         if (!isFresh(fiveMinute, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt(), FIVE_MINUTE_MAX_AGE)) {
  83:             return ValidationResult.reject("MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION",
  84:                     "No fresh 5m confirmation was available for the 1m BUY signal.");
  85:         }
  86: 
  87:         TradeSignal oneHour = latestAtOrBefore(executionSignal, TREND_INTERVAL);
  88:         if (!isFresh(oneHour, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt(), ONE_HOUR_MAX_AGE)) {
  89:             return ValidationResult.reject("MISSING_1H_CONTEXT",
  90:                     "No fresh 1h strategic context was available for the 1m BUY signal.");
  91:         }
  92: 
  93:         HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment=htfAssessment.assess(fiveMinute,true,oneHour,true);
  94:         if(!assessment.allowed()) {
  95:             recordAssessmentMetric(assessment);
  96:             audit(executionSignal, "DIRECT_BUY", assessment, assessment.code(), "FIX-154 higher-timeframe assessment rejected this BUY.");
  97:             return ValidationResult.reject(assessment.code(), "FIX-154 higher-timeframe assessment rejected this BUY.");
  98:         }
  99: 
 100:         ExecutionProfile profile = ExecutionProfile.from(settings.getExecutionProfile());
 101:         ValidationResult profileResult = switch (profile) {
 102:             case CONSERVATIVE -> conservativeBuy(assessment.effectiveFiveMinute(), assessment.effectiveOneHour());
 103:             // FIX-112B: only the fresh direct-BUY BALANCED path receives the narrow
 104:             // 5m-NEUTRAL exception. validateBuyContext() and AGGRESSIVE fallback keep
 105:             // calling balancedBuy() directly so their proven Production behavior is unchanged.
 106:             case BALANCED -> balancedBuyWithNeutralFiveException(
 107:                     executionSignal, assessment.effectiveFiveMinute(), assessment.effectiveOneHour(), entryQualityScore);
 108:             case AGGRESSIVE -> aggressiveBuy(executionSignal, assessment.effectiveFiveMinute(), assessment.effectiveOneHour());
 109:         };
 110:         ValidationResult result = cap(profileResult, assessment);
 111:         audit(executionSignal, "DIRECT_BUY", assessment, result.code(), result.explanation());
 112:         return result;
 113:     }

  37: 
  38:     public void record(TradeSignal reference, String path,
  39:                        HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment,
  40:                        String finalResult, String reason) {
  41:         // Replay signals are JDBC-generated and have no entity id. Replay parity
  42:         // uses its own evidence tables; never write a misleading Production audit.
  43:         if (reference == null || assessment == null || reference.getId() == null
  44:                 || (replayScope != null && replayScope.active())) return;
  45:         AuditPayload payload = AuditPayload.from(reference, path, assessment, finalResult, reason);
  46:         try {
  47:             executor.execute(() -> persist(payload));
  48:         } catch (RejectedExecutionException rejected) {
  49:             metrics.auditWriteFailed();
  50:             log.warn("FIX-154 assessment audit queue is full; BUY decision remains unchanged: path={}, referenceSignalId={}",
  51:                     path, reference.getId());
  52:         }
  53:     }
  54:

  60:                       one_hour_signal_id, one_hour_raw_decision, one_hour_effective_decision,
  61:                       one_hour_candle_close_time, one_hour_age_seconds, one_hour_data_quality_valid, one_hour_usability_reason,
  62:                       five_minute_signal_id, five_minute_raw_decision, five_minute_effective_decision,
  63:                       five_minute_candle_close_time, five_minute_age_seconds, five_minute_data_quality_valid, five_minute_usability_reason,
  64:                       confidence_threshold, assessment_band, hard_confirmation_present, max_initial_percent,
  65:                       legacy_veto_result, final_result, final_reason)
  66:                     VALUES (?, ?, ?, FALSE, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
  67:                     """,
  68:                     p.referenceId, p.path, Timestamp.from(p.evaluatedAt),
  69:                     p.one.id, p.one.decision, p.effectiveOne, timestamp(p.one.candleClose), p.one.ageSeconds, p.one.quality, p.one.usability,
  70:                     p.five.id, p.five.decision, p.effectiveFive, timestamp(p.five.candleClose), p.five.ageSeconds, p.five.quality, p.five.usability,
  71:                     HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.HARD_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD, p.band, p.hardConfirmationPresent, p.maxInitialPercent,
  72:                     p.legacyVeto, p.finalResult, p.reason);
  73:         } catch (Exception e) {

  76:                     p.path, p.referenceId, e);

  83:         static Context from(TradeSignal signal, Instant evaluatedAt) {

===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md:1 =====
   1: # Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign
   2: 
   3: **Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
   4: **Status:** DRAFT ƒ?" NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
   5: **Created:** 2026-10-10  
   6: **Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review
   7: 
   8: ## 1. Business objective
   9: 
  10: Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
  11: as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
  12: isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.
  13: 
  14: The exact intended flow is:
  15: 
  16: ```text
  17: historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  18:   -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  19:   -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  20:   -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  21:   -> replay-local position lifecycle
  22:   -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  23:   -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
  24: ```
  25: 
  26: Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
  27: `signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.
  28: 
  29: The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
  30: network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
  31: wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
  32: claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
  33: remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.
  34: 
  35: ## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence
  36: 
  37: The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:
  38: 
  39: 1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
  40:    use several shared Production services.
  41: 2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
  42:    positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
  43:    labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
  44:    does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
  45: 3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
  46:    event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
  47:    position was open.
  48: 4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
  49:    `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
  50:    Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
  51:    were not retained.
  52: 5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
  53:    have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
  54: 6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
  55:    path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
  56:    live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
  57:    isolation.
  58: 
  59: ## 3. Boundaries
  60: 
  61: ### In scope
  62: 
  63: - Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
  64: - Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  65:   daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
  66: - Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
  67: - Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
  68: - Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
  69: - Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.
  70: 
  71: ### Out of scope
  72: 
  73: - Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
  74: - Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
  75: - Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  76:   external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
  77: - FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
  78: - Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.
  79: 
  80: ## 4. Contractual modes
  81: 
  82: | Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
  83: |---|---|---|---|
  84: | `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
  85: | `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
  86: | `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |
  87: 
  88: The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
  89: must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.
  90: 
  91: ## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility
  92: 
  93: A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
  94: state are written unless all of the following hold:
  95: 
  96: 1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
  97: 2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
  98:    USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
  99:    limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
 100:    and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
 101: 3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
 102:    global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
 103:    that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
 104: 4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
 105:    symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
 106:    current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
 107: 5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
 108:    position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
 109:    `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
 110:    consumed range.
 111: 6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
 112:    enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
 113: 7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
 114:    enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
 115:    derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this

===== agent-docs/RPR-0.4_REVIEW_RESPONSE.md:1 =====
   1: # RPR-0.4 independent-review response
   2: 
   3: **Date:** 2026-10-10  
   4: **Status:** Analysis response only ƒ?" RPR-0.4 remains rejected ƒ?" no implementation authorised  
   5: **Inputs reviewed:** Coordinator run `20261010-095027`, Claude rounds 1ƒ?"3 and Codex cross-examination.
   6: 
   7: ## 1. Conclusion
   8: 
   9: The independent review correctly found that RPR-0.4 is a useful direction but
  10: is not a complete implementation contract. It must not be implemented as
  11: written. This response does not amend or approve RPR-0.4; it records the
  12: required shape of a replacement revision (RPR-0.5 or later).
  13: 
  14: ## 2. Accepted material findings and required correction
  15: 
  16: | ID | Accepted finding | Required correction in the next complete specification |
  17: |---|---|---|
  18: | CR-01 | The change registry is too high-level to reconcile implementation. | Supply a registry with one ID per artifact: exact file/class/method, before/after behaviour, configuration key, database migration/table/column/index, log event/level/context, tests, documentation, rollback and Production/Replay effect. |
  19: | CR-02 | The proposal changes shared Production classes even if the intended business-policy change is Replay-only. | Replace the claim ƒ?oProduction is read-only/no rollbackƒ?? with a per-item impact statement. Any shared extraction or evidence capture must have Production regression tests, transaction/latency analysis and a code rollback path. No intended production trading-policy change is permitted. |
  20: | CR-03 | Current Replay misses production orchestration, not only wallet state. | Register the close-message/price-event lineage, label-free profit-lock exit, advisory stop-loss, SELL/add suppression during an active profit lock, progressive-add gate, analysis/fill clocks, historical-only/skipped candles, same-boundary higher-timeframe visibility and daily-budget timing. Each must be either implemented for parity, classified as an allowed divergence, or cause a parity refusal. |
  21: | CR-04 | ƒ?oParityƒ?? has no usable oracle after the virtual account diverges from Production. | Define separate outcomes: (a) **decision parity**, compared with stored as-of Production decision evidence; (b) **lifecycle parity**, only for an authorised matching virtual starting state; and (c) **simulation**, useful output that must never claim parity. Classify scheduling/concurrency differences explicitly rather than silently accepting them. |
  22: | CR-05 | Evidence qualification is incomplete and incorrectly treats deliberate exclusions as failures. | Define an era boundary and eligible evidence set. A parity run must use the same accepted/applied price observations Production could use; `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` remain deliberate exclusions, not missing evidence. Require as-of candle identity/provenance, signal mode/outcome, configuration/version, sentiment availability time and applicable order-book/derivative evidence. A gap definition belongs in simulation diagnostics; parity refuses only when required accepted evidence is absent. |
  23: | CR-06 | Default `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` is not currently reachable for evidence eras with derivatives/configuration history missing. | Do not make an impossible mode the default. The replacement must either (1) register forward capture of the missing evidence and make parity available only from that capture date, or (2) require the caller to choose `SIMULATION` explicitly and reject a parity request with exact reasons. |
  24: | CR-07 | The base revision, migration sequencing and deployment facts conflict. | Freeze an exact Git commit/diff identity before design approval. Reconcile the observed migration/code state with AGENTS.md and actual Flyway history using read-only evidence. The replacement spec must state the base commit, migration numbers reserved, merge order, target database compatibility and the outcome if an expected table/migration is absent. |
  25: | CR-08 | Multi-symbol/global-budget ordering is not designed. | Either restrict the first parity scope to one virtual account/symbol with an explicit refusal for multi-symbol runs, or register deterministic cross-symbol scheduling and global-limit claim ordering in `ExecutionReplayScope` with tests. |
  26: | CR-09 | Replay-only removals are product decisions, not automatic parity repairs. | Do not remove FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, the pre-FIX122 selector, or `EXPERIMENTAL` mode in the parity change unless their callers, persisted data and user-facing workflows are inventoried and the user approves their retirement. Until then, isolate them from baseline parity and make their outcome clearly experimental. |
  27: 
  28: ## 3. Additional source findings to preserve in the next scope
  29: 
  30: The independent review identified source-backed gaps that must become registry
  31: items rather than prose aspirations:
  32: 
  33: - Replay currently can analyse a `closed=0` candle and use an earlier candle
  34:   snapshot. A parity run needs durable candle identity/finality checks.
  35: - The live close path and Replay do not necessarily see the same 1m decision
  36:   at a candle boundary. Event lineage, not a loose timestamp sort, must define
  37:   the replay ordering rule.
  38: - Production can subsequently close an active profit lock without rechecking
  39:   labels, while current Replay may retain the position. It also has an advisory
  40:   signal-path stop-loss that Replay does not invoke.
  41: - Historical derivatives/order-book/sentiment evidence may be unavailable or
  42:   have only event time rather than record/availability time. Unknown evidence
  43:   must produce a diagnostic refusal for parity, not a no-look-ahead claim.
  44: - A Replay account must never read Production `wallet_*` state. The forbidden
  45:   scope must name the Production tables precisely while allowing Replay-owned
  46:   test/result tables where approved.
  47: - A manual replay run executes in the same JVM and database pool as the trader.
  48:   Its resource limits and cancellation behaviour must protect Production
  49:   freshness and must be tested.
  50: 
  51: ## 4. Non-material corrections to include
  52: 
  53: - Include the current configuration values that affect sizing and daily limits,
  54:   including `max-open-positions` and `max-daily-loss-percent`.
  55: - Define behaviour when the Production daily-budget check would fail or throw.
  56: - Clear `RegimeStateService` replay state deterministically after every run.
  57: - State whether any new Replay evidence pre-write extends or replaces the
  58:   current pending-evidence guard.
  59: - Document that current paper trades use zero fees, so parity is not a claim
  60:   about exchange-net profitability.
  61: - Test cancellation/worker completion, two-request admission behaviour,
  62:   curated and random/incident windows, look-ahead, and refusal-rate reporting.
  63: 
  64: ## 5. Required next review package
  65: 
  66: Before another document gate, provide one exact revised specification and no
  67: implementation. It must include:
  68: 
  69: 1. A frozen source baseline and migration/deployment reconciliation.
  70: 2. The full artifact registry described in CR-01.
  71: 3. The three parity levels and their explicit oracles/refusal conditions.
  72: 4. The evidence-era and availability-time model.
  73: 5. The single-symbol-first or deterministic multi-symbol decision.
  74: 6. A user decision table for each proposed Replay-only retirement.
  75: 7. A complete test/validation/rollback/documentation plan.
  76: 
  77: Until then, `RPR-0.4-DRAFT` remains **REJECTED** and no source, database,
  78: configuration or deployment change is authorised.

 413:     private ValidationResult cap(ValidationResult result, HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment) {
 414:         if (!result.allowed() || assessment.maxInitialPercent()==null) return result;
 415:         int capped=Math.min(result.positionPercent(),assessment.maxInitialPercent());

 156:     /**
 157:      * FIX-043 production recovery path. Persists a missing historical signal while forcing
 158:      * every time-sensitive context lookup (sentiment, fundamentals, MTF/BTC context, etc.)
 159:      * to the recovered candle's original close/evaluation time.
 160:      *
 161:      * This is deliberately NOT equivalent to analyze(indicator), which uses Instant.now().
 162:      * Using current context to reconstruct a 12:21 candle at 12:30 would create a synthetic
 163:      * signal that Production could never have produced at 12:21 and would break Replay parity.
 164:      * The same buildSignal(...) implementation is shared; only temporal authority differs.
 165:      */
 166:     @Transactional
 167:     public TradeSignal analyzeRecovered(TechnicalIndicator indicator, Instant evaluationTime) {
 168:         if (indicator == null) {
 169:             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Recovered technical indicator is required");
 170:         }
 171:         IndicatorSnapshot snapshot = toSnapshot(indicator);
 172:         Instant asOf = evaluationTime == null ? snapshot.candleOpenTime() : evaluationTime;
 173:         TradeSignal signal = buildSignal(snapshot, asOf, true, false);
 174:         signal.setSignalMode("HISTORICAL_ONLY");
 175:         return signalRepository.save(signal);
 176:     }
 177: 
 178:     /**
 179:      * Regression-only analysis path. Runs the same production scoring/final-decision
 180:      * code but returns an unsaved TradeSignal so replay can never write to trade_signal.

 182:     @Transactional(readOnly = true)
 183:     public TradeSignal analyzeForRegression(IndicatorSnapshot snapshot, Instant evaluationTime) {
 184:         if (snapshot == null) {
 185:             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Regression indicator snapshot is required");
 186:         }
 187:         ReplayAnalysisProfiler profiler = replayAnalysisProfiler.get();
 188:         long startedNs = profiler == null ? 0L : System.nanoTime();
 189:         try {
 190:             return buildSignal(snapshot, evaluationTime == null ? snapshot.candleOpenTime() : evaluationTime, true, true);
 191:         } finally {
 192:             if (profiler != null) {
 193:                 profiler.recordCall(System.nanoTime() - startedNs);
 194:             }
 195:         }
 196:     }

  22:     private final AnalysisScoringProperties properties;
  23: 
  24:     @Transactional(readOnly = true)
  25:     public CandleDataQualityResult validate(String symbol, String intervalCode) {
  26:         int minimum = properties.dataQuality().minimumCandles();
  27:         List<Candle> candles = new ArrayList<>(candleRepository.findClosedCandles(
  28:                 symbol, intervalCode, PageRequest.of(0, minimum)
  29:         ));
  30:         candles.sort(Comparator.comparing(Candle::getOpenTime));
  31: 
  32:         List<String> warnings = new ArrayList<>();
  33:         int invalid = 0;
  34:         for (Candle candle : candles) {
  35:             boolean invalidOhlc = candle.getOpenPrice() == null
  36:                     || candle.getHighPrice() == null
  37:                     || candle.getLowPrice() == null
  38:                     || candle.getClosePrice() == null
  39:                     || candle.getVolume() == null
  40:                     || candle.getOpenPrice().signum() <= 0
  41:                     || candle.getHighPrice().compareTo(candle.getLowPrice()) < 0
  42:                     || candle.getClosePrice().compareTo(candle.getLowPrice()) < 0
  43:                     || candle.getClosePrice().compareTo(candle.getHighPrice()) > 0
  44:                     || candle.getVolume().signum() < 0;
  45:             if (invalidOhlc) {
  46:                 invalid++;
  47:             }
  48:         }
  49: 
  50:         Duration expectedStep = intervalDuration(intervalCode);
  51:         int missing = 0;
  52:         if (!expectedStep.isZero()) {
  53:             for (int index = 1; index < candles.size(); index++) {
  54:                 Duration actual = Duration.between(candles.get(index - 1).getOpenTime(), candles.get(index).getOpenTime());
  55:                 if (actual.compareTo(expectedStep) > 0) {
  56:                     long steps = actual.toMillis() / expectedStep.toMillis();
  57:                     missing += Math.max(0, (int) steps - 1);
  58:                 }
  59:             }
  60:         } else {
  61:             warnings.add("Unsupported interval for gap validation: " + intervalCode);
  62:         }
  63: 
  64:         if (candles.size() < minimum) {
  65:             warnings.add("Insufficient history: " + candles.size() + "/" + minimum + " closed candles");
  66:         }
  67:         if (missing > 0) {
  68:             warnings.add("Missing " + missing + " candle(s) in the analysis window");
  69:         }
  70:         if (invalid > 0) {
  71:             warnings.add("Found " + invalid + " candle(s) with invalid OHLCV data");
  72:         }
  73: 
  74:         boolean valid = candles.size() >= minimum
  75:                 && missing <= properties.dataQuality().maximumMissingCandles()
  76:                 && (!properties.dataQuality().blockOnInvalidOhlc() || invalid == 0);
  77: 
  78:         return new CandleDataQualityResult(valid, minimum, candles.size(), missing, invalid, List.copyOf(warnings));
  79:     }
  80:

 304: ## Do Not Start From Zero
 305: 
 306: Existing agreed knowledge should be used as established project context.
 307: 
 308: Agents SHOULD NOT repeatedly rediscover established architecture without
 309: reason.
 310: 
 311: However, persistent documentation is not unquestionable truth.
 312: 
 313: If current code, database evidence, logs, tests, or runtime behavior
 314: contradict persistent knowledge:
 315: 
 316: 1. Report the contradiction.
 317: 2. Do NOT silently accept the old documentation.
 318: 3. Investigate the discrepancy.
 319: 4. Have both agents review the new evidence.
 320: 5. Update shared knowledge only through the dual-agent agreement process.
 321: 
 322: # MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT
 323: 
 324: This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
 325: project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.
 326: 
 327: For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
 328: produced and approved the same exact revision of:
 329: 
 330: `agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`
 331: 
 332: The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:
 333: 
 334: 1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
 335: 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
 336: 3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
 337: 4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
 338:    migration/test/documentation).
 339: 5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
 340:    behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
 341:    transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
 342: 6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
 343:    make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
 344:    credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
 345:    candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
 346: 7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
 347:    NONE when there is no impact.
 348: 8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
 349:    failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
 350:    validation.
 351: 9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
 352: 10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
 353:     log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.
 354: 
 355: No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
 356: registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
 357: may be hidden inside an approved fix.
 358: 
 359: ## Dual-agent specification approval
 360: 
 361: The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
 362: approved by BOTH agents:
 363: 
 364: - Codex: APPROVED
 365: - Claude: APPROVED
 366: - Unresolved material disagreements: 0
 367: 
 368: There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
 369: revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
 370: both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
 371: until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.
 372: 
 373: ## Code implementation direction
 374: 
 375: After explicit user approval:
 376: 
 377: - Codex is the implementation owner.
 378: - Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
 379: - Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
 380: - Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
 381:   Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
 382: - Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
 383: - Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
 384:   overrule a material Claude objection.
 385: - Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
 386:   approval and requires another complete Claude review.
 387: - A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
 388:   reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
 389:   requires renewed user approval.
 390: 
 391: ## Post-implementation registry reconciliation
 392: 
 393: Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
 394: approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
 395: actual change must map to an approved registry ID.
 396: 
 397: Acceptance requires at minimum:
 398: 
 399: - missing registered changes: NONE
 400: - unregistered implementation changes: NONE
 401: - unresolved material review findings: 0
 402: - required logging implemented and reviewed
 403: - required tests/validation completed with evidence
 404: - Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
 405: - required documentation updates completed
 406: - Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
 407: - Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED
 408: 
 409: # POST-CONSENSUS PERSISTENT KNOWLEDGE
 410: 
 411: After an investigation reaches evidence-backed convergence, durable agreed
 412: facts are candidates for `agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md`. Candidate text must
 413: be the SAME exact revision reviewed by both Codex and Claude and may be written
 414: there only when BOTH return AGREED. Rejected hypotheses, unresolved disputes,
 415: speculation, and proposed-but-not-implemented architecture must not be stored
 416: as confirmed current state.
 417: 
 418: After an approved implementation is fully reviewed and validated, update the
 419: appropriate Codex and Claude project-memory documents and the shared agreed
 420: knowledge/decisions so future sessions do not start from zero. These updates
 480: The PDF must identify the exact revision, SHA-256 of the source Markdown, Codex verdict, Claude verdict, and implementation-authorization state. Any material source edit invalidates the frozen revision and requires both agents to review the new revision again. PDF generation never authorizes implementation.
 481: 
 482: ## Evidence-driven round continuation
 483: 
 484: A configured MaxRounds value is an emergency/cost ceiling only. It is never a consensus criterion. `MaxRounds=0` means no fixed coordinator round ceiling. If a positive ceiling is reached with material disagreement, status is CONSENSUS NOT ACHIEVED and implementation remains blocked. Continue only through a new/continued formal run with preserved evidence; never manufacture agreement.
 485: 
 486: ## Post-consensus structured knowledge harvest
 487: 
 488: After formal convergence and dual approval of the exact technical review, the Coordinator must harvest a structured candidate knowledge package from the completed evidence. Candidate categories may include CONFIRMED_IMPLEMENTATION, CONFIRMED_BEHAVIOR, CONFIRMED_ARCHITECTURE, CONFIRMED_TRADING_RULE, DATABASE_ENTITY_FACTS, OPERATIONAL_LOG_FACTS, HISTORICAL_FACT, and REJECTED_ASSUMPTIONS. The SAME exact candidate text must be independently approved by Codex and Claude before it enters AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md. Proposed future design is never recorded as implemented architecture.
 489: 
 490: ## Executable implementation review loop
 491: 
 492: `scripts/implementation-review.ps1` is the guarded automation entry point for the approved code phase. It MUST refuse to run unless explicit `-UserAuthorized` is supplied for the exact frozen specification. Codex is implementer; Claude is mandatory independent full reviewer. Claude never edits implementation in this loop. Rejection returns to Codex correction, then Claude reviews the complete updated implementation again. A material design deviation stops the loop and requires a revised specification plus renewed dual-agent and user approval. No deployment is authorized by this script.

===== src/main/java/com/crypto/config/CandleAnalysisAsyncConfig.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.config;
   2: 
   3: import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
   4: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
   5: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
   6: import org.springframework.scheduling.concurrent.ThreadPoolTaskExecutor;
   7: 
   8: import java.util.concurrent.Executor;
   9: import java.util.concurrent.ThreadPoolExecutor;
  10: 
  11: /**
  12:  * FIX-043: dedicated executor for closed-candle analysis.
  13:  *
  14:  * Production evidence on 22 Aug 2026 showed 96.7-100% 1m candle persistence but only
  15:  * 17-20% technical_indicator/trade_signal coverage. The old AFTER_COMMIT listener ran the
  16:  * complete indicator -> signal -> wallet path synchronously on the Binance message thread.
  17:  * When that work took longer than the incoming market-data cadence, the websocket consumer
  18:  * could not keep up and the recovery scheduler became the de-facto analysis clock.
  19:  *
  20:  * CandleAnalysisDispatcher supplies serial bounded admission per symbol+interval while this pool supplies
  21:  * parallelism across independent streams. The pool sizes are configuration-driven because the
  22:  * correct concurrency depends on VPS CPU/DB capacity; changing them must never change trading
  23:  * decisions, only how quickly independent market streams can be analyzed.
  24:  *
  25:  * FIX-153: saturation rejects admission; the dispatcher releases its reservation and the
  26:  * local detector can retry. Analysis must never execute on a Binance ingestion thread.
  27:  *
  28:  * IMPORTANT: this class must never contain BUY/SELL scoring, execution thresholds,
  29:  * SETUP_CONFIRMATION_WAKEUP, ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE or exit logic.
  30:  */
  31: @Configuration
  32: public class CandleAnalysisAsyncConfig {
  33: 
  34:     @Bean(name = "candleAnalysisExecutor")
  35:     public Executor candleAnalysisExecutor(
  36:             @Value("${trading.analysis-worker-core-size:8}") int coreSize,
  37:             @Value("${trading.analysis-worker-max-size:16}") int maxSize,
  38:             @Value("${trading.analysis-worker-queue-capacity:200}") int queueCapacity
  39:     ) {
  40:         int safeCoreSize = Math.max(1, coreSize);
  41:         int safeMaxSize = Math.max(safeCoreSize, maxSize);
  42:         ThreadPoolTaskExecutor executor = new ThreadPoolTaskExecutor();
  43:         executor.setCorePoolSize(safeCoreSize);
  44:         executor.setMaxPoolSize(safeMaxSize);
  45:         executor.setQueueCapacity(Math.max(1, queueCapacity));
  46:         executor.setThreadNamePrefix("candle-analysis-");
  47:         executor.setRejectedExecutionHandler(new ThreadPoolExecutor.AbortPolicy());
  48:         executor.setWaitForTasksToCompleteOnShutdown(true);
  49:         executor.setAwaitTerminationSeconds(30);
  50:         executor.initialize();
  51:         return executor;
  52:     }
  53: }

===== src/main/java/com/crypto/config/SharedAnalysisExecutorConfig.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.config;
   2: 
   3: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
   4: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
   5: import org.springframework.scheduling.concurrent.ThreadPoolTaskExecutor;
   6: import java.util.concurrent.ThreadPoolExecutor;
   7: 
   8: /** FIX-138: reserve live capacity; historical catch-up cannot consume it.
   9:  * No caller-runs fallback: a saturated pool must never execute on a dispatch clock. */
  10: @Configuration
  11: public class SharedAnalysisExecutorConfig {
  12:     // FIX-142: dispatcher and executor receive the same validated historical setting.
  13:     @org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value("${shared-market.analysis.historical-threads:1}")
  14:     private int historicalThreads=1;
  15:     @Bean("sharedLiveAnalysisExecutor")
  16:     public ThreadPoolTaskExecutor live() { return pool(8, "shared-live-"); }
  17: 
  18:     @Bean("sharedHistoricalAnalysisExecutor")
  19:     public ThreadPoolTaskExecutor historical() { if(historicalThreads<1||historicalThreads>4)throw new IllegalArgumentException("Historical capacity must be 1..4");return pool(historicalThreads, "shared-history-"); }
  20: 
  21:     private ThreadPoolTaskExecutor pool(int size, String prefix) {
  22:         var executor = new ThreadPoolTaskExecutor();
  23:         executor.setCorePoolSize(size);
  24:         executor.setMaxPoolSize(size);
  25:         // At most one admitted task per thread; the small queue only accommodates
  26:         // the interval between a task releasing its lane and returning to the pool.
  27:         executor.setQueueCapacity(size);
  28:         executor.setThreadNamePrefix(prefix);
  29:         executor.setRejectedExecutionHandler(new ThreadPoolExecutor.AbortPolicy());
  30:         executor.setWaitForTasksToCompleteOnShutdown(true);
  31:         executor.setAwaitTerminationSeconds(30);
  32:         return executor;
  33:     }
  34: }

===== src/main/java/com/crypto/config/Fix154AuditAsyncConfig.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.config;
   2: 
   3: import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
   4: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
   5: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
   6: import org.springframework.scheduling.concurrent.ThreadPoolTaskExecutor;
   7: 
   8: import java.util.concurrent.Executor;
   9: import java.util.concurrent.ThreadPoolExecutor;
  10: 
  11: /** Audit evidence must never borrow the connection held by an execution path. */
  12: @Configuration
  13: public class Fix154AuditAsyncConfig {
  14:     @Bean(name = "fix154AuditExecutor")
  15:     public Executor fix154AuditExecutor(@Value("${fix154.audit.queue-capacity:500}") int queueCapacity) {
  16:         var executor = new ThreadPoolTaskExecutor();
  17:         executor.setThreadNamePrefix("fix154-audit-");
  18:         executor.setCorePoolSize(1);
  19:         executor.setMaxPoolSize(1);
  20:         executor.setQueueCapacity(Math.max(1, queueCapacity));
  21:         executor.setRejectedExecutionHandler(new ThreadPoolExecutor.AbortPolicy());
  22:         executor.initialize();
  23:         return executor;
  24:     }
  25: }

## OBJECTIONS

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Move the exact approved revision to the canonical contract path, or amend governance first; freeze commit plus dirty-worktree manifest before design approval.

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Add a complete dependency/read/write matrix and registry entries for the request, controller, service, worker, all Replay-owned tables, surviving audit writes, observer paths, `WalletAutoExecutionService` or a deliberately extracted virtual equivalent, FIX-154, price-event lineage, migration/test infrastructure, and every shared-class rollback path.

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Define atomic staging/publication, terminal states separate from claim labels, cancellation/crash handling, and all existing-column mappings.

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Specify separate signal-path and live-price-path order, decision/fill clocks, boundary event ordering, daily-budget/loss semantics, and higher-timeframe visibility. Preserve a documented divergence only when its output is simulation-only.

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Replace HTTP minimum-notional lookup with immutable profile constraints, identify the fail-closed guard location and thread propagation, and test that no Production account read/write or HTTP call can escape it.

- **[AGENT INFERENCE]** Define the analysis-parity oracle and an eligibility/refusal matrix for candle finality, as-of data quality, derivatives, order book, sentiment, signal mode, price evidence, and FIX-154 assessment inputs.
